**Final status, September 10: the approved OPT00–OPT36 optimization program is complete and the exact qualified candidate is active locally.** Every required execution gate is closed. All thirty-seven outcomes are accounted for; selected changes are implemented and tested, rejected candidates stay disabled, and explicitly prerequisite-dependent research remains deferred. This is completion of the approved program, not a claim that every possible future optimization has been implemented.

**Session record and later live-server benchmark.** The completed session's exact final closure is [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-10-optimization-final-program-complete]]. The original work, all-item outcomes, accepted and rejected experiments, failed runs, resource/thermal scheduling corrections and activation history remain preserved below. The later three-prompt benchmark, every clean timing, explicit discarded attempts and observed-versus-estimated tokens/sec are now documented separately in [[records/measurements/user-server-throughput-2026-09-10]]. It does not change the original qualification or the conservative calibration decision.

The actual working source matches all 150 qualified source inputs. The installed executable SHA-256 is `9268e4b2a3371e78a71d493d7788559a06a22498e8061a89278c4918c6764673`; the installed Metal library also matches the qualified asset. Installed defaults match the final qualification, and a bounded generation check returns exactly `OK`. The previous installation remains available for rollback. Resource admission, cleanup, free model lock and unchanged Git index checks pass, with no owned model/compiler left running. Nothing is staged, committed, pushed or released by this activation: [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-10-optimization-final-local-activation-and-all37-outcomes]].

Final reconciliation checks the complete candidate matrix, source and artifact identities, all thirty-seven historical dispositions, empirical calibration, public claims and generated documentation. Database validation has zero errors and one unchanged historical `LOG_UNKNOWN_KIND` warning at `log.md:124`; it is not reported as warning-free: [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-10-optimization-final-preactivation-closure]]. The installed activation adds a final delivery check to those already-passed gates.

It preserves expert residency and all numerical shapes/operations. Added native assertions observe zero retained disposable buffers and test direct success, cancellation, checked expert-read error, state rollback and exact retry. Initial execution evidence (preserved history): [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-initial-implementation]] and [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-second-implementation]]. The generator now has an experimental bounded read-scope transaction that preserves the existing compute schedule and restores a coherent checkpoint on cancellation, with tiled MTP consumption and final-pass admission. Its corrected native checks passed 593 scope lifecycle, 240 synthetic image-span/MTP recovery and 112 MTP first-entry/tail mechanics assertions; see [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-scope-generation-and-mtp]]. The first lifecycle helper was invalid (zero output means maximum allowance in this API), was terminated during seed decode, and is preserved with the correction. The first real 7,961-token pilot reduced expert reads from 45,018 to 18,753 but its only clean pair was 22.5% slower; two reference cells had swap activity and both pairs were excluded. The 256-token tile is not adopted. Both 2,048-token routed tiles and the later 4,096-token tile plus compact frontier passed 117 numerical, 593 lifecycle and 240 synthetic image/MTP assertions on their own frozen executables. Neither pilot produced a clean performance pair because of swap activity. See [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-larger-routed-tile]] and [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-large-tile-and-frontier]]. Workspace GPU-wait/CPU-sort counters are now populated; all timing claims from older zero counters remain explicitly unavailable. The bounded chronological-prefill calibration fitted the existing expected peak at 8,192 tokens but could not advance to longer contexts because every attempt had swap activity. V6 adds per-generator VM interval snapshots and an opt-in real-server observation path. Three short server A/A pairs passed without swap; every long chronological-prefill pair was excluded. Exact evidence and limits are in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-prefill-family-and-serving-intervals]]. No planner constants changed. Future timing blocks avoid concurrent workspace shell commands as an additional contamination control; swap exclusion is unchanged. All read-scope controls remain off by default. Live TCP stall/Gateway integration and latency nonregression remain open. The output queue narrowly missed its client nonregression threshold. Neither is adopted; [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-router-selection]] preserves the exact gate results and 702 refreshed ordinary-generation assertions. No whole OPT item is marked complete from its first substep, and no combined speedup or release readiness is claimed.


**Correctness correction discovered during OPT10, 2026-09-05.** The first dense-visibility component gate exposed an existing sparse-mask error: tensor `/` promotes Int32 positions to float32, so the old current-block-tail expression becomes `qp + 1` and drops every partial current block. [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-indexer-partial-tail-counterexample]] preserves the 32 failing assertions, source binding and independent pinned-MLX reproduction. The unconditional `floorDivide` correction passed 262 component assertions, including an independent scalar retained-key oracle; the dense-only and bounded-plus-dense full-model continuation gates each passed 1,127 assertions. The corrected 8,192-token read-scope comparison passed all 117 assertions. Both new catalogues passed all 24 T0/T1 groups. Exact evidence is in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-corrected-mask-and-shared-overlap]]. The optional dense shortcut also conservatively checks operands against NaN/infinity/overflow before skipping selection. Every new long-context optimization comparison must bind a corrected reference; historical exact equivalence to a frozen buggy reference is not intended-model parity. Earlier below-threshold ownership and terminal-forward measurements are not invalidated by this mask change.

**OPT19 shared substep:** the projection-only prelaunch passed 1,128 exact state/continuation assertions. Four clean short-request pairs showed a 1.66% median client reduction, but the declared gate required five clean pairs and at least 5%; the swap-contaminated first pair stays excluded. No adoption follows. V129 now implements bounded one-token resident-routed overlap and passes native state/lifetime tests; its frozen serving comparison and GPU-event proof remain pending. The linked corrected-mask/shared-overlap run preserves the failed qualification and the new host-condition/harness observations.

Sharing reads while retaining the original 256-token compute schedule passed 808 exact state/route/continuation assertions at each of 1,024, 2,051 and 4,096 tokens. Its code-review pilot has one swap-clean exact-output pair (50.43 to 20.84 seconds) but all candidate peaks exceed the 10 GB target, so it fails qualification; two other pairs remain swap-excluded ([[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-exact-256-compute-scopes]]). Compact frontier and then piecewise workspace writes preserve exactness but still fail the memory limit. The phase trace puts the peak in routed computation/reduction, so smaller routed tiles are the next bounded candidate. The block selector's denormal counterexample, corrected 163/43 component gates and 1,128 integrated block-selector state assertions are preserved in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-denormals-and-workspace-peak]]. Isolated selection savings do not qualify inference; MTP/long-context/real-request gates remain open. Request-entry kernel preparation and no-op phase attribution are being hardened. No memory target, numerical gate or production default was relaxed.

**V22–V25 continuation:** reducing routed tiles to 1,024 retained all 808 exact 4,096-token scope assertions and passed the one-cell 10 GB resource gate at 9,612,759,144 sampled bytes. Its new paired development run produced no clean pair: three reference intervals had swap activity, the third also became thermally fair, and the run was stopped during round 4 after five valid pairs were no longer possible. This is a failed/incomplete qualification, not a speed claim; [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-smaller-routed-tile]] preserves it. The cached-prefix MTP selector test passed 514 assertions with actual main/head dispatch and exact continued state. Request-local image reuse passed 64 real-tower assertions before the successor added processor/tower/padding identities for prefix safety; [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-mtp-selector-and-image-reuse]] preserves those exact versions and ten host-only harness tests. Cross-request image caching/startup and image serving qualification remain open. The 72-wide attention prototype passes 60 pinned Python/Float64 component assertions: width 128 is the more promising measured large-shape candidate, while width 80 saves memory but is slower. These exploratory component timings do not certify inference; Swift/full-tower/quality/request gates remain open ([[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-vision-padding-probe]]). The memory battery now uses structured sampled footprint rather than a rounded CLI value that can miss a transient peak. No production optimization or planner family has been activated.

Objective: improve time to first token, steady decode latency, peak memory and memory efficiency across the pinned model's text, tool-use and image paths. Preserve model quality, cache correctness, numerical contracts, bounded memory, cancellation, serving APIs and independent CLI use. Optimize these jointly at explicit memory budgets; there is no universal fastest configuration for every workload.

Evidence: [[records/analyses/2026-09-04-first-principles-inference-audit]], including its three captured raw runs. Source starting point: `b88fdb2cf1f4070c838bccac59911ad2625d25bb`; recheck current source and dependency identities before implementation. The native state-compaction probes establish allocator savings and final-logit equality on two synthetic prompts. Their timings are excluded from performance claims.

### One program, with a detailed prefill chapter

This is the **single optimization program**, now 37 OPT items across seven stages. [[records/plan/n6-prefill-bound-the-pass-then-read-each-expert-once]] is its detailed prefill chapter. Its phase and P1–P5 ownership map points to the OPT items here; it is not a second implementation queue. This record owns execution status, dependency order and experiment lifecycle. N6 owns the detailed prefill algorithms, workspace alternatives and per-phase gates. Keep one raw-run/measurement ledger and link results from both views.

The former independent execution sequences are superseded by this program. Consolidation cancels no optimization work: overlapping mechanisms have one OPT owner, while N6 retains their detailed design and evidence. The historical audit remains supporting evidence. Resolve any future disagreement between the program and its detail chapter in both records before implementing the affected item; do not silently choose the weaker acceptance criterion.

The combined source review is captured in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-04-prefill-unification-first-principles]]. Current source identity is `6e66a367f93e636c3729179e24007aa99c97b9a6`; the inference tree and package pins still match the original audit. This review adds source-derived opportunities, not production changes or measured speedups.

**The preferred strategy is to combine read reuse with bounded compute and remove work before tuning it.** Start with compact state ownership and remaining-aware scheduling. Then make indexer/attention, PLE, expert rows and reduction storage explicitly bounded. Compare read-scope workspace alternatives at the actual memory target. Integrate independently winning selection, shared/resident overlap, attention and GDN kernels; remeasure their combination because they can compete for the same resources or remove the same wait. Prefer measured end-to-end latency and capacity over the largest scope or most concurrency.

The merge resolves five real planning gaps:

- Disjoint MoE output placement still costs N×K×H. N6 U1 now compares canonical full output, deterministic numerical fold and one-layer weight workspace explicitly; a compact fold is not assumed to preserve the existing reduction tree.
- OPT36 targets the pinned backend's full sorts behind `argPartition` in router and indexer, with ordered IDs and tie behavior gated separately from scoring changes.
- OPT35 removes unused final mixing and investigates final-layer query/MoE pruning when full multis are not required. It preserves every state-producing operation and disables the terminal pruning for MTP/full-output diagnostics.
- OPT09/10 include compact raw-tail ownership, absolute-visibility dense shortcuts and fused indexer score preparation, with explicit rollback and numerical gates.
- OPT19/26 probes no longer wait unnecessarily for the full read-scope redesign. Prefill lifecycle, tail warming and progress fixes have explicit owners; capacity improvements use a memory-benefit plus latency-nonregression gate.

Read N6 U1–U5 for the derivations, alternatives and test cases. This overview deliberately does not maintain another copy of their byte tables or performance numbers. Both chapters are projected into PLAN.md, with this program overview first.

### Execution rules and completion states

Items without an explicit current status remain **unstarted**. On work, mark an item **in progress**, then **verified**, **rejected with evidence**, or **blocked with a concrete dependency**. A completed prototype alone is not verified. An optimization is kept only after its correctness contract and intended resource/latency benefit pass. A candidate that fails is a completed investigation, not a reason to leave an unhelpful feature in the default path.

Ship work in small independent changes, with a known reference path while an experiment is active. Do not stack unverified changes or regenerate numerical goldens simply to accept a discrepancy. For performance candidates, keep typed controls default off during experiments and retain the verified reference arm through qualification and one release after adoption; then remove temporary switches and redundant implementations unless measured hardware/shape differences require dispatch. Correctness/reporting fixes need no switch restoring incorrect behavior. Preserve public diagnostic APIs independently of experimental flag cleanup. Repeat checks after further changes only where those changes affect the previous result.

A verified memory/capacity optimization can be adopted on explicit resource benefit with latency nonregression; it need not wait for a later scope redesign to deliver a speedup. A speed claim still requires measured request latency improvement. No blanket speed multiplier is promised. Freeze each experiment's primary metric, minimum useful benefit, allowed regressions and repetition count **before** running it. Choose thresholds from an A/A noise characterization and the intended workload. Report failed and inconclusive results, including memory gains that did not translate to physical-footprint or latency gains.

### Dependency and fallback semantics

The stages express priority; they are not a requirement to finish every item in one stage before starting an independent item in the next. Dependency IDs refer to required **capabilities and evidence**, not an assumption that every preceding optimization succeeds. A rejected accelerator may be replaced by the existing validated path when that path still satisfies the dependent item's memory, state and correctness contract; name that fallback in the result. Do not waive a necessary bound because a candidate was rejected. This is particularly relevant to output tiling before larger read scopes and to pool lifetime proofs before asynchronous writes.

OPT08 means initial accounting for accepted early changes. Its later recalibrations are acceptance checks attached to those changes, not a dependency cycle that waits for the entire program. OPT03/23/24 are conditional on MTP availability; OPT25 can first improve output-budget handling and mode policy using the existing verified recording/reconciliation implementation. It does not depend on both recording and reconciliation accelerators winning. Missing vision fixtures likewise do not block unrelated text work.

The starting baseline must be reconstructible, not merely hashed: retain an exact buildable source/dependency snapshot or a commit plus the exact relevant patch, and the baseline executable/metallib identities. Preserve unrelated uncommitted work. Changes to a shared prerequisite require rerunning the dependent checks they affect. Failed candidates retain their experiment evidence and can be removed or disabled cleanly without requiring users to downgrade model artifacts.

V25/V26 correctness and the unresolved vision memory failure are captured in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-vision-identity-and-router-weights]]. Padded attention passes 60 Swift component assertions and the unchanged full-tower reference bands. Image identity/reuse passes 68 native checks; image serving passes 22 correctness checks but samples peaks of 10.336 and 10.043 GB above its 10 GB target. The corrected same-geometry image-content test passes four checks after preserving an earlier ineligible-prefix test failure. Router-weight promotion passes 30 component, 1,130 main-state and 370 MTP assertions; the frozen V26 catalogue passes 27 groups. The extra 251.66 MB main-router / 256.90 MB main-plus-head storage is reported, not yet qualified at fixed total memory. OPT17 is in progress. A first-image resident reservation and governor policy correction are under test; no new default or performance claim is earned.

V27/V28 evidence is in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-vision-budget-and-cache-bookkeeping]]. First-image resident memory is now reserved inside a target-driven plan, allocator cache is bounded before vision encoding, and governor replans preserve resident features/context under one generation lock. The 26-check image sequence passes correctness; all recorded peaks are below 9 GB but four generator intervals have swap and remain excluded from strict resource qualification. A new separate first-image preparation observer passes a two-request bounded check with clean swap counters and maxima below 7.55 GB. The low-target refusal preserves the existing pool/prefix plan and subsequent text generation. These do not qualify maximum image resolution or sustained resource bounds. The direct expert index, touched-slot pin list and FIFO ring pass 1,163 main-state and 367 MTP checks plus the 28-group catalogue. All seven short-serving pairs are valid and exact, but median latency regresses 0.08248% and only three improve; the frozen speed criterion fails and controls stay off. Large-pool and full n-gram eviction effects, asynchronous read recovery and memory epochs remain open.

The separately frozen 701-token code-prefill workload completed seven paired rounds; five clean exact-output pairs all slowed down (median 9.26105%) despite the first pair reading 25.704 GB fewer expert bytes. Rounds 3 and 7 are swap-excluded. The scope remains off for this workload and the crossover point is still unqualified; see [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-shorter-prefill-workspace-counterexample]]. OPT22 now has a checked-read foundation, bounds/overflow checks and interrupted/short-read fault seams. V29 passes the 29-group catalogue and 702 real-model generation assertions. Current synchronous callers still fail closed on nonrecoverable read errors; request-level recovery, async publication and stale completions remain open, as explicitly recorded in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-checked-read-foundation]]. The governor cannot silently remove the memory charge for a head it has not unloaded. OPT18 pointwise normalization fusion passes component/full-state/MTP gates, but six valid serving pairs show a 0.04850% median latency regression and only three improve; it stays off. V32 corrects and passes the future-head reference/candidate comparison (368 assertions). OPT22 owned tensor descriptors pass nine lifetime/bounds checks, 1,130 full-model assertions and 370 MTP assertions. Five valid serving pairs show a 0.01280% median latency regression and only two improve, so direct handles also stay off. These reject the measured short-serving speed hypotheses, not the ownership and checked-read foundation. Evidence: [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-compiled-normalization]] and [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-owned-read-handles]].

**OPT10/15 V33–V36:** compact block IDs pass exact reference-mask reconstruction, including exceptional scores and partial blocks. The D256 online-softmax prototype passes 208 corrected component assertions but fails 52 of 807 full-model numerical assertions. Five clean component pairs per shape are 4.84×, 21.98× and 7.07× slower at the three declared dense/sparse shapes despite lower allocator peaks. This scalar kernel is rejected; bounded native attention remains the dependency path. Short queries retain their exact existing path. A matrix-tiled successor with preserved BF16 rounding remains a separate conditional candidate, with no implied quality or speed result. Failed initialization, short-query and test-scope revisions are preserved in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-selected-attention-rejection]].

**OPT35 V37/V38:** explicit terminal demand passes two 810-check numerical families (2,051/4,096 tokens), 462 cancellation/image-span/full-output lifecycle assertions and 370 MTP-exclusion assertions. State/upstream routing is exact; final-row MoE logits satisfy the existing numerical bands. Seven clean real-serving pairs produce identical output and a 2.02711% median client improvement, with six improving and round 7 regressing 9.10707%. The frozen 5% speed gate fails, so the prototype stays off. It removes intermediate terminal queries and unused MoE rows; final-pass QSA query pruning and full quality/integration gates remain open. Evidence: [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-terminal-prefill-demand]].

### Stage 0 — establish trustworthy instruments and baselines

| Item | Concrete deliverable and code surface | Dependencies | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| OPT00 — reproducible benchmark manifest | Freeze production/dependency hashes, actual token IDs, image hashes/preprocessing settings, sampler seeds, context and output lengths, MTP setting, cache state, pool and total-memory limits. Repair and reuse `Tools/prefill_bench.sh` under N6 Phase 0, including immutable fixtures, arm interleaving, fail-closed metrics, effective-override accounting and a real serving cell; build this harness once. | None | A/A establishes variability; all results identify cold/warm filesystem, cold/warm expert pool and prefix reuse separately. No model-speed result inferred from the earlier contended probes. |
| OPT01 — correct timing and memory observations | Add request-stage timing and optional sampled physical-footprint high-water tracking in `ProcessMemory.swift`, `Generate.swift`, `Engine.swift`, `ExpertStore.swift` and `Server.swift`. Include queue, images, text prefill, first byte, steady decode, speculative reconciliation, socket stalls, read bytes/counts and GPU waits. | OPT00 | Verify timer boundaries and overhead against disabled instrumentation. Report RSS, sampled physical footprint, allocator active/cache/peak separately; never derive GPU time as wall minus overlapping I/O. |

Start performance work with existing bounded 8.1–10 GB targets; a fixed smaller diagnostic pool may be used when its footprint is separately bounded. Before every heavy step inspect running model processes and reclaimable memory. Run one model process at a time and keep several GB headroom. Use existing policy simulation for governor checks, not memory-hog stress. No context-cap or measured scheduling-bound increase belongs in this stage.

The baseline manifest covers nonrepeating prose, code, tool exchanges, short length-capped replies, long outputs, reused and edited prefixes, and known-content images. Run only the subset relevant to an individual change; reserve the integrated matrix for Stage 6. Begin long-context tests at a fitting rung and advance to 8k/32k only when measured headroom permits.

| Item | Concrete deliverable and code surface | Dependencies | Acceptance |

Handle ordinary prefill, subsequent decode and speculative state references. | OPT00; OPT01 for memory claims | Exact cache values and logits for ownership-only changes; state persists correctly into further tokens. Reproduce allocator reduction, measure footprint over time, test cancellation and recording, and remove full-chunk retention across layers. |
| OPT03 — compact MTP retained rows | Materialize the retained final multi-stream row in `MTP.swift` and audit checkpoint/rollback slice ownership in `Model.swift`/`Generate.swift`. | OPT02 | Exact retained values and continued logits; no stale or oversized parent in normal prefill, acceptance/rejection, cancellation or prefix storage. Do not claim that `eval(slice)` copies it. |

Final-scope expert admission stays correct; N6 3.1 tests rebalanced or explicitly swept short prefill tails against decode-pool churn, with numerical and fixed-suffix gates. |
| OPT05 — avoid unused last forward | Stop ordinary decode after the last permitted emission without calculating unused next-token logits. Preserve the pending/unconsumed token in exact prefix accounting. | OPT00 | One-token/zero-token/length/EOS/stop/disconnect cases are correct; next-turn reuse consumes the pending token exactly once. Lower completion latency where this forward was wasted. |
| OPT06 — compact sampler work | In `Generate.swift`, reduce candidate sorting/CDF work and CPU synchronizations. Reserve active growth on prefix hits as well as misses, include rollback coexistence and price effective overrides; N6 1.4/2/3.4 define the affected cases. | OPT01, OPT02, OPT04; OPT03 when MTP is enabled. Refresh after later memory-layout changes | Charge actual owned capacities without double-counting shared buffers. Regenerate the whole estimator family together, including throughput/cost coupling; simulated policy tests, fitting real targets and derived projections agree. Do not spend a theoretical allocator saving before real footprint is verified. |

Stage exit: accepted ownership changes are correct across continued state, the extra tail pass and final forward have dispositioned results, and memory reporting/planning reflects measured behavior. This stage is useful independently of custom kernels.

OPT15/19/26/36 fixed-input or schedule-only probes can proceed before OPT16. Their production integration requires the relevant ownership, numerical and memory gates. Initially keep the existing chronological GDN in a new scope path, then compare any independently accepted OPT26 implementation. OPT35's unused-mixer substep is also independent. This permits earlier experiments, not concurrent model processes.

Stage exit: N6's deterministic record-count and memory gates pass; the selected scope/tile policy improves its declared workloads at fixed total memory. Recalibrate OPT08 after choosing the implementation. Do not extrapolate uniform expert routing, an I/O residual, or a single read-scope bound into universal throughput.

| Item | Concrete deliverable and code surface | Dependencies | Acceptance |

Report regressions and startup/steady-state tradeoffs. No unattended large-memory tests. |
| OPT33 — coherent planner, artifacts and delivery readiness | Finalize estimator calibration, compatibility/fallback dispatch, temporary-flag cleanup, run records, measurement sections, claims and generated projections. | OPT32 and refreshed OPT08 | One coherent measured parameter family; source/dependency and weight identities recorded; standalone CLI/server compatibility maintained. Build/static/brain/projection/claim checks pass. Record hardware coverage limits; local M5 evidence does not certify every Mac. |
| OPT34 — disposition all broader alternatives | Maintain the register below with measured outcomes or explicit revisit conditions. | OPT00; later profiles identify any useful successor | Every audit recommendation has an implemented, rejected, conditional or deferred outcome. Quality-changing alternatives remain distinct from equivalent runtime optimization. The plan never marks an unexplored candidate as a demonstrated improvement. |

Completion of OPT33 makes an implementation ready for delivery; this planning task does not claim implementation, benchmarking or release completion. Subsequent performance work starts from the measured remaining bottleneck rather than automatically enabling every prototype.

### Broader-alternative register for OPT34

| Candidate | Conditional/deferred disposition and reason to revisit |
|---|---|
| Lower-bit expert weights, reduced top-K, expert pruning, smaller/different base model | Separate quality/compute comparison. Revisit with frozen task-quality criteria and byte math; never call deleted model contributions an equivalent optimization. |
| BF16/quantized recurrent state or quantized KV | Quality-sensitive. Revisit only if measured state/KV bandwidth or capacity warrants it; GDN drift requires long-sequence tests. |
| Lossless expert compression | Low-priority experiment after read reuse/layout. Revisit with representative data, codec/decode cost and end-to-end traffic measurements; the limited compressed-weight sample is insufficient. |
| BF16-dequantized hot experts | Conditional reuse-heavy compute experiment; charge lost cache capacity and conversion time. |
| Streaming more resident weights / row-caching embeddings | Conditional bounded prototype: source confirms separate lookup embedding and dense lm_head. Exact packed lookup payload357,580,800B;8192packed rows11,796,480B. Theoretical payload difference345,784,320B (125expert records), before buffers/overheads. No planner credit until checked main/MTP reads, exact row parity, public tensor compatibility, resource and fixed-total-memory serving gates pass. |
| mmap-based storage / direct CPU-to-GPU arena writes | Mapping alone does not manage residency. Any useful direct-write variant belongs under OPT20/22 and must prove lifetimes. |
| Cross-layer expert prediction/read-ahead | Conditional; requires measured prediction utility and bounded wasted reads/staging, since exact routing is not available early. |
| Another draft model or speculative tree | Conditional after OPT25; charge extra memory and acceptance/verification cost. |
| Faster/multiple SSDs or another compute device | Hardware study only if measured storage/compute bounds justify it. Existing hardware results do not predict latency for another machine. |
| Activation spill for a larger read scope | Conditional OPT16/34 alternative after in-memory scopes; charge every frontier write/read, disk space, cleanup and prompt-data custody. No once-read total-traffic claim that excludes spill. |
| Full frontend/backend language rewrite | Deferred unless a profile identifies a concrete bottleneck the targeted OPT18/20 changes cannot address. |
| Lower image resolution | Explicit input-quality tradeoff; separate from OPT27's kernel equivalence. |
| Energy optimization | Measure joules per emitted token and useful request when a reliable measurement method is available; no energy-efficiency claim from elapsed time alone. |
| Weight hosting/download changes | Cold-install scope, not steady inference. Revisit only from a measured install/startup bottleneck; OPT28 profiles the relevant cold path. |

### Test and evidence contract

Existing commands verified in source include `Tools/sampler_gates.sh`, `Tools/planner_gates.sh`, `slotstream prefix-check`, `sweep-check`, `elastic-check`, `mtp-check`, `mtp-parity`, `context-check`, and `Tools/verify.sh`. Use their actual option parsing at implementation time. Optional MTP/vision fixtures must be present before claiming their gates passed. A missing optional fixture does not block text-only work, but an affected feature cannot be marked verified or enabled by default until its checks run. Keep missing-fixture skips explicit. `Tools/verify.sh` is a final integrated gate, not something to rerun after each prose or low-impact change.

Add focused checks for mechanisms not covered today: ownership/retention across continued tokens, per-request footprint sampling, exact tail scheduling, indexer block rollback, compact sampler ties, deterministic tiled output placement, event-safe pool mutation, final-token prefix accounting, and bounded slow-client backpressure. Each check must exercise a behavior that could regress, not merely mirror the implementation.

Two numerical classes govern acceptance:

- **Unchanged arithmetic:** ownership, byte layout, admission, scheduling of independent operations and lookup changes must retain exact values/routing where operation shapes and arithmetic remain the same. Check full state when the change touches it, not only the final answer.
- **Changed grouping/kernels:** prefill rechunking, fused attention, projection/reduction changes and recurrence reformulations need the existing rechunking control plus state/logit/routing/quality checks. Matching greedy text alone is insufficient; do not hide drift by refreshing goldens.

For each kept candidate, capture the baseline/candidate command, binary/source/dependency identities, run order, input identities, total-memory settings, VM state, primary metric and full result. Use interleaved paired runs and report medians plus tails with the A/A comparison. Mark loaded/swapping runs discarded as timing evidence. Measure both fixed pool (mechanism) and fixed total memory (product effect) when the candidate changes resident storage. Cache-content invariance and prefix/MTP correctness are separate from numerical differences caused by batching.

### Whole-engine interaction and recovery contract

These are mandatory acceptance details for the affected OPT items and OPT32. Several are already specified for prefill in N6; they now apply to the combined runtime as well.

**MTP validity is a state invariant, not a non-null pointer.** Current `Generate.swift` selects a cached draft state using `state.mtp != nil`, while the plain decode path advances the main model without updating that draft cache. This was designed for a fixed serving mode. OPT25 must not introduce dynamic off/on transitions on top of that assumption. Track whether draft state covers the exact consumed main prefix, including the required previous multi-stream row. Disabling speculation must either keep draft state correctly maintained or invalidate/discard it. Re-enabling requires a fresh state or an explicitly bounded, verified reconstruction; a stale non-null cache is never eligible. Start with request-boundary mode selection. A later mid-request fallback may occur only at a committed verification boundary and must establish the validity state for the next request. Include the cost of maintenance, rebuild, resident head and any displaced expert slots in the policy measurement.

**Only a coherent whole-model boundary may be published as reusable state.** During layer-major work, different layers may cover different token counts. A progress percentage is not a reusable prefix. On cancellation/error, restore the last committed boundary or discard the request state, join owned jobs, and restore recording mode, allocator limits, admission flags and offsets. Do not resume partially advanced layers or cache them as complete tokens. N6 defines the bounded scope checkpoint strategy. Pool resize and numerical/state-layout changes occur at compatible committed boundaries or after abort-and-restore, not merely when the host has temporarily stopped launching work.

**Queued output is distinct from delivered output and consumed tokens.** OPT05/30 must track these separately. A socket failure after enqueue must cancel work promptly without duplicating emitted tokens or storing a prefix the model did not consume. Preserve tool-call/UTF-8/stop-sequence framing and completion reasons. Report time to first token ready, first protocol delta, and first visible answer text separately; a reasoning/special token need not be user-visible output.

**Derived layouts and asynchronous reads need failure recovery.** OPT20/22 must test short/interrupted reads, read errors and stale completions after cancellation/resize. Stage completion publishes a slot mapping only after the complete intended tensor record is ready. Attach pool/scope generation identity so an old completion cannot overwrite a recycled slot. An interrupted repack or disk-full condition leaves the verified original checkpoint usable: construct into temporary storage, verify lengths/content/layout/model/quantization identity, then atomically publish the completed artifact/manifest. A stale or corrupt derived layout is rejected; safe fallback uses the verified original only when the request can restart from a coherent state. Simulate failures through bounded seams, not by filling the user's disk or corrupting original weights.

| Interaction to exercise | Required invariant | Owning items |
|---|---|---|
| Prefix reuse, MTP on → off → on, including a length-capped pending token | Main/draft offsets and previous multi agree, or the draft cache is explicitly invalid and cannot be reused | OPT03/05/24/25/29 |
| Layer-major prefill canceled in each layer family and across an image span | Restore/discard at a whole-stack boundary; retry has correct tokens, image identities and n-gram history | OPT02/09/14/16/29/30 |
| Speculative rejection across a four-token indexer boundary and a KV capacity-growth boundary | Exact accepted prefix, raw tail, completed block cache and storage ownership survive trim | OPT09/23–25 |
| Pool shrink or canceled scope with delayed I/O completion | No stale completion, mutation of a GPU reader's slot, leaked pin or unjoined worker | OPT19–22/30 |
| Queue saturation, split UTF-8 or tool-call output, stop sequence and disconnect | Bounded buffers; exact token/byte framing; consistent consumed/pending/delivered accounting | OPT05/06/30 |
| Repeated requests, image changes, prefix eviction and regrowth | Memory reaches a bounded steady state at fixed maxima; no stale feature/state reuse | OPT02/03/07–09/28–30 |
| Optional MTP absent, image feature disabled, and capability-specific kernel unavailable | A validated supported path remains; skipped feature checks are not claimed as passes | OPT15/18/23/25/27/33 |

Use cheap state/shape/fault-seam checks for combinatorial coverage and representative bounded real-model runs for integration. A bounded repeated-request soak is needed to catch lifetime growth that a single prefill misses; freeze its duration and request sequence before running it. This is not a large-memory stress experiment.

### Benchmark comparability and hardware coverage

OPT00 freezes both development workloads and a separate confirmation set before tuning. Do not select policies or regenerate fixtures based on the confirmation results; a changed policy gets a newly declared confirmation protocol. Use a fixed teacher-forced decode suffix as a mechanism control and real free generation as the product/quality check. They answer different questions. Count actual output tokens and EOS/stop behavior; different generated continuations are not identical decode work. An all-hit test must demonstrate actual hit counts with a fitting working set or a clearly labeled layer-level replay; it must not allocate the entire expert bank on this Mac.

A new process does not establish a cold filesystem cache. Label cache state from the actual setup and observations; do not globally purge the user's machine to manufacture a cold run. Record power mode, power source, thermal state where available, background load, warmup and sustained-run conditions. Freeze repetition and tail-estimation requirements after the A/A pilot; do not report a reliable p95 from too few independent observations or treat correlated token samples as independent runs. Predeclare nonregression limits for affected short/long, warm/cold, text/image and sampled/greedy cases, not just the primary winning cell.

Report the physical-footprint sampling interval and its overhead. A sampled high-water mark can miss short peaks: combine it with event-boundary/live-allocation accounting and conservative memory bounds rather than claiming continuous peak coverage. Keep allocator memory and process measurements distinct.

The public package currently declares macOS 14 support, and shader loading distinguishes macOS 14, 15 and 26 families (`Package.swift`, `Tools/fetch_metallib.sh`). A new kernel must be gated by actual supported capabilities/shapes with a tested existing fallback. Test dispatch and initialization failure before state mutation. Do not switch to a fallback and continue a possibly corrupted request after a GPU execution failure. Validate known short-query/head-dimension/tile boundaries and separately confirm the packaged metallib/JIT path. Local M5 timing is scoped to that machine; activation on an untested older GPU/OS remains restricted until compatibility evidence exists. Core text improvements that retain the established portable path need not wait for optional GPU-specific accelerators.

Kernel changes can preserve algebra while changing softmax, normalization or reduction rounding. For OPT06, first remove redundant sorting/synchronization while retaining normalization/CDF behavior where required by the exact sampler contract; a compact reduction is a separate numerical candidate. Likewise, an upstream MLX alternative must be pinned and evaluated independently, not mixed into a kernel comparison as an unrecorded dependency upgrade.

### Coverage review and readiness limit

All audit areas have an owner: cost/measurement (OPT00/01/08), retained state (OPT02/03), scheduling and read reuse (OPT04/12–16), attention/indexer/RoPE and selection (OPT09–11/15/36), decode/router/storage (OPT17–22), GDN/MTP (OPT23–26), n-grams (OPT07/14/22), generation/sampling and unused prefill work (OPT05/06/35), prefix/governor/serving (OPT08/29–31), vision/startup (OPT27/28), and broader alternatives/delivery (OPT32–34).

The latest review unifies N6 and the whole-engine program, adds OPT35/36, makes the reduction-storage alternatives explicit and removes artificial probe dependencies. Earlier MTP validity, recovery, benchmark and hardware gates remain binding. The first ownership/instrument slice remains appropriate; the following independent probes now have a clearer route. Readiness means that the identified work has a sound implementation and validation route. Numerical tolerances, practical kernel wins, final scope sizes and combined latency gains are still experiments; they cannot be certified by reviewing a plan.

### First implementation slice

1. Freeze OPT00's small representative baseline and add OPT01's bounded observations.
2. Implement OPT02, then verify exact continued-state behavior and footprint before recalibrating any memory allowance.
3. Extend the ownership correction to OPT03 when the MTP fixture is available; otherwise keep that feature explicitly unverified and proceed with text-only work.
4. Implement OPT04 as a separate change; validate the existing scheduling envelope and actual pass/read counts.
5. Land or reject each result independently. Add OPT35's unused-mixer substep and OPT36 fixed-score probes alongside OPT05/06/09; test P1/P2 overlap, attention and GDN according to the measured exposed stalls.
6. Once local bounds pass, compare N6 U1 workspaces under OPT16 at matched memory, then integrate winning kernels/overlap and run a combined A/B. Record each OPT status here and its detailed evidence in N6 without counting predecessor gains twice.

The starting point, dependency order, tests and completion criteria are defined. Execution can begin without another planning pass; experimental branches are resolved by their stated measurements rather than assumed to be improvements.

The V38–V41 all-hit investigation is preserved in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-all-hit-compute]]. A 24 GB reference calibration proves the selected request can be all-hit, but the cross-process comparison delivered no candidate cells because the unchanged 30/30.3 GB headroom requirement did not fit. Its V39 bounded-wait successor started no model; a 22 GB V40 calibration still read experts and cannot qualify as all-hit. The bounded V41 full-model next-token replay instead fits 640 slots and passes 109 checks, including exact logits/routes and zero expert reads in every measured cell. All five candidate pairs are valid: router caching reduces median replay time by 2.08716%, compiled normalization by 0.562315%, each with four positive pairs. Both fail the unchanged 5% gain threshold and remain off. This closes the missing small-pool all-hit mechanism measurement for these prototypes; fixed-total memory, cold-start and broader compute fusions remain unqualified. No production integration or whole-program completion is claimed.

OPT28 source preparation now has bounded metadata admission before CGImage creation/EXIF rotation, a cumulative one-GiB source charge, checked resize errors, failure cleanup and exact request-local source sharing under the default-off image-reuse control. V42–V44 raw evidence is [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-image-source-admission]]. The failed initial CLI invocation and overly specific malformed-PNG assertion are retained. The corrected catalogue passes all thirty groups (132 vision assertions); failure recovery passes twelve checks, source refusals pass nine live checks across Ollama/OpenAI/Gateway, and both source-reuse serving sequences pass twelve checks. The preserved JPEG pixels and full tower embeddings are byte-identical. Duplicate preparation counts change from two to one without sharing different image bytes; the reduced admission charge is not a physical-memory or latency gain claim. Maximum-image resource qualification, actual allocator/GPU failure and integrated adoption remain open.

OPT21 now fixes duplicate missing-key slot ownership and validates expert keys and total pin capacity before mutation; the checked API exposes bounded request errors while the existing API stays source-compatible. V45 passes 160 real-weight checks across container modes and resize/retry boundaries, plus all thirty catalogue groups. V46 serving retains six clean exact pairs after one swap-excluded pair and passes the explicitly frozen 5% latency non-regression gate (median reduction 0.423885%, not a speed claim). Raw evidence, the failed V45 protocol delivery and the sixteen-test cross-binary harness are preserved in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-duplicate-pool-requests]]. Checkpoint I/O recovery, asynchronous completion lifetimes, admission policy and integrated defaults remain open.

OPT06 now corrects the exact-zero inverse-CDF boundary: the frozen predecessor selected a filtered-out token for a valid seed, while the corrected path preserves RNG evolution and returns the only legal top-k-one token. The independent oracle and all 647 sampler assertions pass. The separate default-off GPU draw path removes one host scalar readback and passes the native component gate: 13.9789% median filtered-sampler reduction alone and 17.4517% with threshold selection, with exact sequences in every pair. Full sampled serving retains all seven clean pairs per candidate and passes the separately frozen 5% latency non-regression gate; whole-request median reductions of 0.1060%/0.1266% are not meaningful speed claims. All evidence, exact identities, seventeen harness tests and the original counterexample are preserved in [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-sampler-device-draw]]. Positive-draw semantics and prior goldens remain intact. Confirmation, MTP integration and production optimization defaults remain open.

### Adaptive speculation V49–V51: lifecycle passes, transparent timing policy rejected

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-adaptive-speculation-counterexample]] preserves 129 initial model assertions, all 31 catalogue groups, the expanded 256/257 result and the subsequent 32-token timing-sensitivity counterexample. Fixed-depth MTP also differs from plain, so this is a batching/reproducibility limit, not a task-quality measurement. The state machine, bounded tails, EOS/callback cancellation and invalidated-head cache lifecycle pass their checks. ADAPTIVE_MTP stays off and the frozen V50 serving study is not run. ### Fixed-schedule MTP serving V51: exact outputs, no accepted request gain

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-fixed-mtp-serving]] preserves seven clean pairs each for first-entry reuse and fused GDN recording at the same 10 GB target and fixed depth one. All twenty-one cells satisfy resource checks and all IDs/text match. ### Runtime allocation V52–V55: protected reservations and measured cache benefit

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-runtime-budget]] preserves 561 planner assertions, all 32 catalogue groups, 24 live lifecycle checks, two predecessor diagnostic failures and both serving studies. The default-off RUNTIME_BUDGET path transfers only unused explicit prefill/prefix reservations to expert slots, persists its policy through metadata/vision/governor changes, and prevents restoring spent reservations through cache toggles. An active correctness fix separately prevents first-image loading from regrowing previously donated capacity. At 10 GB, the plan moves from 961 to 1217 slots while retaining the previous expected-peak envelope; constants and full active-context charges stay unchanged. Seven clean exact-output 8.1 GB development pairs all improve, with median 4.9062062% client reduction and decode records 4722 → 4177, but miss the frozen 5% gain gate. The independent 10 GB chat confirmation has four clean exact pairs after round-four swap exclusion, median 4.1457244% reduction, and fails both five-pair and gain requirements. No replacements run; the option remains off. Larger-target prefill reservation recovery and full OPT08/integrated qualification remain open.

### Cache admission V56–V58: rejected broad policies and a bounded successor

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-cache-policy-screen]] preserves exact replay scripts, trace bytes and all results. Bounded frequency eviction fails five of six cells. Broad layer-local replacement reduces reads at 640 slots but regresses the larger-capacity development cases, so neither broad policy enters inference. A newly frozen 640-slot-only successor passes two fresh reference trace screens after replay exactly reproduces actual decode records, including prefill warmth: compiler 21414 records and containers 20280, with predicted reductions 5.7486% and 6.3166%. These are causal offline record predictions, not wall-time or quality claims. V59 starts a default-off native FLOOR_CACHE path restricted to exactly 640 slots and ordinary demand loads; larger pools and staged sweep admission retain CLOCK. Its state, lifecycle and performance qualification remain open.


### Native floor cache V59–V60: correctness passes, serving gate fails

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-native-floor-cache]] preserves 226 pure victim, 1128 state assertions at each of 255/256 tokens, 367 MTP, 33 actual allocation-boundary checks and all 33 catalogue groups. ### Responsive governor V61–V63: safe boundaries and overhead qualified

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-responsive-governor]] preserves the default-off pressure latch, nonblocking busy poll, explicit-plan immunity, queued allocation refusal, safe cancellation/resize, floor prefix/cache release and serialized lifecycle. All 34 catalogue groups and 59 live lifecycle assertions in each of plain/MTP mode pass, including partial scope rollback, chronological prefill, streamed/nonstreamed decode, exact retry and stop while the governor waits for generation. Controlled scope pressure-to-boundary observations are 5.52/6.85 ms; streamed callbacks observe pressure in microseconds after avoiding an unnecessary next forward. These are diagnostic observations, not hardware preemption guarantees. V62 serving failed before inference from a malformed arm envelope; the unchanged seven-pair design runs as delivery correction V63. Six clean exact-output pairs pass the declared 5% median overhead limit with 0.3313024% median client regression; round two remains swap-excluded. No request speedup is claimed. ### Compact main indexer history V64–V67: ownership and recovery pass; resource study inconclusive

At 8192 component tokens the indexer allocation is 589824 versus 2621440 bytes (77.5% lower); at 2051 model tokens total allocated sequence bytes fall by 7077888 (8.3333%). The frozen 2509-token code resource study has zero clean pairs because the first three reference intervals swap; it stops for futility during the third candidate once five clean pairs become impossible. Candidate allocation observations match actual MLX active savings, but no process-memory or latency qualification follows. The separate 20 GB runtime-reservation calibration has 4834 slots and still performs 2933 prefill plus 1479 decode reads, so it fails its all-hit criterion without a larger-cap retry. Compiler delivery failures, partial cells and all source identities are preserved. The harness suite passes 18 tests after arm-schema validation and 19 after per-pair allocation gating. No reservation or default changes; real resource qualification, vision-boundary integration and full acceptance remain open.


### Default-path acceptance V68–V69: broad correctness passes; long-memory qualification remains open

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-default-acceptance]] preserves the complete frozen default-path verification and targeted harness corrections. V68 passes 21 top-level gates, including all model hashes, numerical and state references, elastic/prefix/sweep/MTP checks, 15 behavioral probes and 25 real image-serving checks. Four failures exposed missing physical sampling in two commands, a long-recall answer truncated inside reasoning, and a Bash 3.2 empty-array failure before API launch. V69 enables sampling, preserves the same long prompt and 16-token limit under the ordinary non-thinking template, requires a completed exact recalled value, and fixes the empty server arguments. Short memory passes at 6,397,939,144 sampled physical bytes; all 74 API robustness checks pass. Long recall passes with 7,972 prompt tokens and four output tokens, but swap excludes the long-memory interval. No replacement or threshold relaxation ran. The 20-test Python harness suite passes. All experimental controls remained default off, so this is default-path acceptance evidence and does not qualify combined optimization defaults or end-to-end speed.


### Actual-offset expert layout V70–V72: qualify a pool-read prototype; reject sweep adoption

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-actual-expert-layout]] preserves the matched per-piece parallel benchmark, byte-verified bounded repack, cache counterexample and cold correction. V70's apparent gains were file-cache measurements: V71 recorded zero packed disk-read bytes for a 5.66 GB requested payload. V72 instead constructs uncached packed and sparse column copies at the original tensor offsets and requires disk-read coverage in every accepted cell. Six bounded fault/identity/atomic-publication tests pass. Seven clean exact pairs each for random batches of 1/4/10 misses show median component reductions of 19.0802305%/16.8597787%/10.6965409%; six pairs for 32 misses show 9.8008323% after one swap exclusion. These pass the frozen component gate, include deinterleave cost, and support a pool-path runtime experiment only. The bounded artifacts occupy 11,337,527,296 filesystem bytes. Dense sweep retains only one cold pair, 25.7104351% slower; subsequent reference cells are cache-resident. Half-density sweep also exceeds the packed scratch physical-memory bound and has no qualified pair. No sweep-packing or runtime default is adopted. Full-model packing, startup verification, failure recovery and end-to-end benefit remain open.


### Synchronous request read recovery V73–V76: lifecycle, HTTP and overhead gates pass

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-request-read-recovery]] preserves checked expert batch/run reads, atomic row-cache insertion, completed-record slot publication and error propagation through ordinary prefill, read scopes, decode and speculative verification. All synchronous workers join before partial staging is freed. On failure the generator drains previous valid pool copies/GPU readers, drops the partially advanced request state, restores allocator limits and resets pins/admission/recording; emitted tokens remain in an explicit error result and partial state is never cached. Existing nonthrowing library APIs remain available; low-level checked callers must discard or checkpoint-restore state after errors.

V73 passes 1,322 real-weight storage/pool recovery assertions and 702 ordinary generation assertions; V74 also passes all 702 generation checks after propagation. V75 passes 151 plain and 160 MTP request-failure assertions, including cache hits, second-pass failure and exact retry. V76 passes 120 real HTTP-handler socket-pair checks across Ollama/OpenAI/Gateway, streamed and JSON responses and both output writers. Every stream error follows delivered text, has valid termination without false success and permits a subsequent request. These are handler-level checks, not TCP admission tests. Both V73 and V76 pass all 35 catalogue groups. The frozen seven-pair fixed-pool serving study retains six clean exact pairs after round-six swap exclusion: median client latency regresses 0.1935286813%, passing the declared 5% non-regression gate. No replacement or speedup claim. Recovering MLX allocator/GPU faults, optional descriptor-open compatibility behavior, asynchronous write epochs, packed runtime layout and combined defaults remain open.


### Packed per-read verification V77: rejected after cold-qualified measurement

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-packed-read-verification]] preserves unchanged V72 criteria with SHA-256 inside every timed packed read. Random batches of 1/4/10/32 misses retain 6/6/5/7 clean exact pairs but regress median component duration 127.5204296%/47.8034520%/21.7805559%/0.9773315%. All speed gates fail. Dense sweep has only one cold pair and sparse sweep none; cache and footprint exclusions remain binding. Six builder tests and two native corrupt/truncated digest failures pass. No replacements, original-weight changes or runtime adoption. Moving verification to load time is still a distinct candidate, requiring measured startup cost, owned-descriptor identity, post-load change rejection and end-to-end benefit.


### Native load-verified expert layout V78–V79: correctness passes, serving and startup reject adoption

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-native-packed-layout]] preserves the native atomic builder, complete load-time verification, config/tensor/source fingerprint, owned descriptor, mutation checks and joined error-to-original recovery. Sweeps remain on original tensor runs. The independent artifact contains 67,947,724,800 payload bytes and occupies 67,947,728,896 disk bytes; construction completed in 54.6058510 s but global swap excludes strict construction cost/resource qualification. All 226 component, 36 catalogue, 993 real-weight storage and 1,130 state/router/rollback assertions at each of 255/256 tokens pass. Plain/MTP failures after delivered text pass 16/18 lifecycle assertions with exact original retry. The 22-test measurement harness now preserves startup separately and gates its amortization estimate on equal work and positive paired steady savings.

All seven exact serving pairs are clean and slower: median client regression 2.96969496% fails both declared speed criteria. Five clean startup pairs add a median 9.6189655 s to ready and 9.7550058 s to the first completed job. No positive steady saving exists to amortize these costs. Experimental packing stays explicitly opt-in and is not adopted; no sweep/default changes or replacements. File stamps detect ordinary changes, not adversarial immutability or silent bit rot between load and read. V72 component gains remain scoped to their cold I/O study and cannot be presented as serving gains. N-gram row/page layout and asynchronous prefill I/O are separate open work.


### Bounded n-gram lookahead V80–V82: exposed waits shrink, full-request speed gate fails

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-ngram-lookahead]] preserves immutable CPU read tickets, exact history/generation binding, original insertion order/accounting, admission within unused cache capacity and cancellation/join through every read syscall. No worker mutates model state or GPU slots. V82 passes 37 catalogue groups, 530 row/cancellation assertions, 1,130 full-state/router/rollback assertions at each of 255/256 tokens, 160 ordinary and 169 speculative failure assertions, and exact layer-zero/one Python parity. V81's scalar-view diagnostic error is retained and corrected without changing the model's arithmetic.

Seven fresh-process 440-token prose pairs are clean and exact. Each arm reads 22,016 expert records; candidates consume 6,736 asynchronous rows. Median exposed n-gram prefetch falls from 119.494917 to 14.428833 ms, but median request time regresses 0.198106304%, failing the 5%/80%-positive gate. Peaks remain 6.562–6.663 GB under the 8.1 GB target. This stage accounts for only about 1.6–2.0% of reference request time. No replacement, warmup relabeling or default adoption. OPT14's lookahead speed hypothesis is rejected for this workload; its bounded PLE tiling and larger-regime qualification remain separate. The next priority is a measured material cost, including the OPT26 recurrence profile and remaining pool handoff/lifetime work.


### V83 — recurrent-layer attribution and OPT26 disposition

Evidence: [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-gdn-phase-profile]]. A package-only observer times GDN preparation, recurrence and finish with explicit evaluation boundaries. Five alternating traced/untraced full-model replays at each of 1/256/1024 tokens pass all 3,600 assertions, including exact logits, ordered routes and every state byte; all extents are complete, nominal and whole-process swap-clean. Catalogue: 37 groups pass. The actual register-resident chronological recurrence remains unchanged.

At 256/1024 tokens, median paired serialized recurrence divided by untraced forward time is 3.078158%/4.486361%, below the frozen 5% materiality screen; tracing overhead is 1.528215%/7.682230%. Existing expert-read time is 1.491718/1.936804 seconds of median 2.334185/3.704381-second untraced forwards. Single-token tracing adds 58.14%, so its phase attribution cannot establish a decode speed opportunity by itself. These are fixed-forward component observations, not request throughput, and phase forcing changes overlap.

**OPT26 disposition: conditionally deferred for the measured 256/1024-token regimes because its material-cost prerequisite is not met.** Revisit after a qualified reduction in read/other costs or a measured larger compute-bound prefill. A successor still needs zero/underflow-safe coefficient construction, partial-block and FP32-state/compensated-reference tests, and end-to-end gains after triangular/intermediate work. No untested chunk algorithm is represented as implemented, rejected by measurement, or faster. Continue OPT20/22 with actual allocation/handoff/scatter attribution before replacing existing managed-buffer staging.


### V84–85 — actual expert staging and scatter attribution

Evidence: [[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-05-optimization-expert-transfer-profile]]. V84's first probe stops before timing on a diagnostic final-pin guard; V85 releases completed-forward bookkeeping pins at the explicit boundary, retaining the guard. V85 then passes 4,860 exact state/logit/router/payload assertions across five clean alternating all-miss forward pairs at each of 1/32/256/1024 tokens. V84's 37 catalogue groups pass. No constructor copy fallback occurs: all tested aligned staging buffers use MLX's managed wrapping.

For one token, untraced replay is 198.756625 ms; forced scatter is 59.483752 ms while allocation/wrapping/eval are only 0.167335/2.699542/0.103377 ms. The frozen transfer-materiality screen passes at 31.316746% for one token and 23.165804% for 32, with 6.301978%/4.588327% tracing overhead. At 256/1024 the sweep performs no pool scatter and total staging fractions are only 0.964122%/0.785319%, failing that screen. These are serialized attribution, not attainable gains; overlapping timings must not be summed twice.

OPT20/22's residual-scatter prerequisite is now demonstrated on bounded all-miss decode/short-prefill replays. First test contiguous slot-range updates when CLOCK reserves one/two destination runs, with ordinary MLX ownership/dependencies and generic scatter fallback. Reusable staging arenas are conditionally deferred in the measured prefill regime; direct CPU-to-GPU writes still require their separate explicit lifetime proof. V124's maximum candidate-only sampled peak was 1,730,627,264 bytes; the subsequent full-reference comparison reached 5,185,652,416 bytes while retaining candidate outputs. They are different correctness intervals, not a valid paired saving.

Three protocols are frozen before execution: independent seven-pair 512-pixel nonregression and 1,024-pixel resource/nonregression comparisons, plus a single maximum-size whole-request feasibility probe. Each pins source/weight/image identities, actual tile counts, preparation plus generator footprint, nominal power/thermal state and zero swap, with strict sampled competing-work checks and no replacements. The 1,024-pixel comparison requires at least 200 MB sampled peak saving in every clean pair; both paired studies require five clean pairs and at most 5 percent median client regression. The maximum probe does not establish a latency effect. All are unrun at this update. Query tiling remains default off and outside integrationCandidate; padding stays rejected.

A further source audit found ordinary-prefix model identity and checkpoint-branch ownership gaps. The unqualified V126/V127 successor adds model-bound state reuse, checked restore/rollback, weak checkpoint validity tickets, and atomic invalid-input rejection tests. V126 failed to compile a diagnostic report call; V127 builds. Native recovery and existing MTP/cancellation regressions are pending, so this is not yet claimed as verified. The new checks retain ancestor checkpoints but reject later snapshots permanently after their KV rows can have been overwritten by another branch.

### V126/V127 — model ownership and checkpoint branch validity

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-06-optimization-state-lineage]] preserves V126's diagnostic compile error and V127's complete build/source closure. V127 passes 43 direct lineage, 151 ordinary read-failure, 160 MTP read-failure, 593 scope cancellation, 240 image/MTP scope, 272 ordinary complete-prompt, 280 MTP complete-prompt and 209 combined vision/MTP assertions (1,948 total). This qualifies the covered recovery behavior; these diagnostic durations are not performance measurements.

State now carries its loaded-model identity; ordinary generator prefix lookup misses a same-geometry foreign model, and checked model entry rejects its state before cache mutation. Prefix forks preserve the model identity but receive independent checkpoint ownership. Checkpoints have weakly registered validity tickets that retain no extra tensor data. A restore or recorded rollback permanently invalidates later tickets even after different tokens grow KV offsets past the old numbers. Older valid ancestors remain restorable. Checked restore/rollback refuses foreign/discarded/incomplete input before mutation; rollback additionally binds the recorded token sequence, base and history-window geometry. Public nonthrowing wrappers remain source-compatible and fail on invalid internal contracts. Discarded snapshots do not accumulate live tickets.

V128 adds a bounded misaligned-draft capture check and an independent, default-off OPT19 resident-overlap prototype. It only partitions the batch axis of one-token QMV operations; larger token batches keep existing grouping, and router-rank reduction order is restored. All requested resident slots are pinned before launch; synchronous read completion is followed by an explicit resident-reader join before any pool write, including read errors. The pinned backend selects gather-QMV from unchanged M/N/K and uses the expert count only as grid depth for this one-token case. The build passes, but native exactness, error-path join evidence and serving benefit are pending. CPU host join time is labeled as such; no actual GPU-overlap event timing or speed claim is inferred from submission order.

### V128/V129 — bounded resident-expert computation during miss reads

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-06-optimization-resident-overlap]] preserves both builds and the unchanged-arithmetic mechanism checks. V128 passes 45 lineage and 597 component assertions; its full-model test passes all numerical assertions but fails the required execution counter because the immediately preceding reference made every candidate one-token call all-hit. V129 explicitly preloads half of each observed reference route for correctness comparisons. It passes 45 lineage, 627 component, 1,128 full-model state/routing/continuation, 151 ordinary read-recovery, 160 MTP read-recovery and 240 image/MTP scope assertions (2,351 total). Two actual mixed-cache read errors each submit resident work, join it exactly once, release temporary pins and retry with exact output. The lineage successor also rejects a checkpoint captured with an unaligned draft cache.

The new RESIDENT_OVERLAP control stays off and outside integrationCandidate. It separates only independent one-token expert QMV batch rows, keeps the original larger-batch path, pins the whole intended request before GPU submission, and joins resident readers after synchronous I/O but before any pool mutation or error unwind. Reassembly preserves original router rank. The pinned backend source is captured: these M=1, at-most-ten-row calls keep the same quantized kernel selection and reduction geometry. The submission/join counters show execution and completed lifetimes; host join seconds do not establish GPU event duration or useful GPU overlap.

The 38-test serving harness now requires matched completed joins for every resident submission. A seven-pair 17-prompt/16-output, 8.1 GB/640-slot protocol is frozen before execution, with exact outputs/eight work counters, minimum five clean pairs, at least 5 percent median client gain, at least 80 percent positive pairs, bounded footprint, nominal power/thermal state, zero swap and no replacements. It is unrun at this update. A serving win still needs combined/event qualification before activation. The three frozen V125 vision studies, complete-prompt confirmation/unique overhead, CPU-copy confirmation, compute islands, remaining prefill qualification and final OPT32/33 are also pending.

### V130/V131 — maximum-image observation and small-image nonregression

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-06-optimization-vision-serving-nonregression]] preserves the maximum-image delivery correction, both complete studies and the V132 measurement successor. The first single-arm maximum protocol was refused before model launch because its sole arm was not named reference; the corrected frozen protocol retains identical candidate settings and explicitly establishes no paired comparison. Its 1,536-pixel request emits the expected Red token, executes 972 query tiles and consumes 2,329 prompt tokens/104,279 expert records. Sampled generation/preparation peaks are 7,016,468,656/6,816,010,416 bytes within the 10 GB plan. Twelve global swap-ins during generation exclude the cell from qualification. It remains a correctness/resource observation with no qualified latency or capacity claim.

The independent seven-pair 512-pixel study completes all fourteen cells. Round one remains swap-excluded; six whole pairs pass the frozen minimum-five-clean / maximum-five-percent median regression gate, with exact token IDs/wire text in every raw pair and unchanged 16,681 prefill records. Median paired client latency regresses **1.15583 percent**; this is a qualified nonregression result, not a speedup. Candidates execute exactly 108 tiles per request, references zero, and qualified candidate sampled generator peaks are 6.7304–6.8032 GB. No replacement round, settings change or output difference occurred. The driver exits 1 to preserve excluded cells while completion.json records the paired gate passing.

A pre-execution audit corrected the resource comparison to take max(generation sampled peak, image-preparation sampled peak) for each process. Both stages already had independent absolute-budget gates, but the saving calculation previously used generation alone; a larger preparation peak could conceal a regression. The new counterexample test proves that case, and all 39 harness tests pass. The unrun 1,024-pixel study receives a new frozen V132 protocol with the original 200 MB-per-clean-pair saving, minimum-five-clean and five-percent nonregression criteria unchanged. No earlier resource claim is rescored to obtain a pass. V125's small-image study had no saving threshold. Query tiling remains off pending larger-image and maximum-size qualification; OPT32/33 and the remaining program are still open.


### V132 — larger-image whole-request resource gate fails

[[sources/runs/2026/09/2026-09-06-optimization-vision-resource-counterexample]] preserves four complete pairs and the interrupted fifth-round reference. Only pair one is clean; pairs two through four are excluded for global swap-ins. At that point no more than four clean pairs could remain in the fixed seven-pair study, below the required five, so the owned driver was stopped for futility and its server cleaned up. There are no replacement rounds or changed thresholds. The completion fails both minimum pairs and minimum sampled peak saving.

The clean pair preserves exact Red output, 50,247 prefill records and 138,922,905,600 read bytes. Query tiling executes 432 tiles. Whole-request sampled peak falls from 6,962,024,432 to 6,859,952,232 bytes: **102,072,200 bytes**, below the required **200,000,000**. Its 15.4488 / 13.7388 second client latencies are an isolated descriptive 11.0692 percent reduction, not a qualified speedup. Image encoding itself is slower (0.6644 / 0.7328 seconds); the observed request difference occurs in prefill with equal expert-read work, and its cause is unproven. All completed raw outputs remain exact and sampled peaks fit the 10 GB plan.

V131's independent small-image nonregression remains valid, while V130 maximum-size serving is swap-excluded and this larger-image resource qualification fails. Query tiling remains off and outside integrationCandidate. Future investigations must be new prospective hypotheses, not revised scoring of this study. Complete-prompt confirmation/unique overhead, resident-overlap and CPU-copy serving, compute islands, remaining prefill qualification, combined acceptance and OPT33 remain open.
